parallel council (decomposed synthesis)\n- `/council-local Rewrite the heritage vetting pipeline` → MaKaLi with local models\n- `@maat P3: Fix the CI pipeline` → Ma'at delegates to P3 Engineering ZONEID constant heritage attribution` → Heritage verification (local deepseek-r1-qwen3-8b)\n\n---\n\n## ⬡ The MaKaLi Triad Architecture (D117)\n\nThe three top-of-pyramid Oversouls form an explicit Transcendent Triad:\n\n```\n                ┌─────────────────────────────┐\n                │   KALI — Transcendent       │\n                │   (Unify, Synthesize,       │\n                │    Return Verdict)          │\n                └──────────────┬──────────────┘\n                               │\n                ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐\n                │                             │\n        ┌───────▼────────┐          ┌────────▼───────┐\n        │  MA'AT — Light │          │ LILITH — Dark  │\n        │  (Build Side)  │          │ (Run Side)     │\n        │  P1-P5 Pillars │          │ P6-P10 Pillars │\n        └────────────────┘          └────────────────┘\n```\n\n### When to Use Each Pattern\n\n| Pattern | Use When | Cost | Benefit |\n|---------|----------|------|---------|\n| **`@kali` direct** | You trust one entity to see all, dispatch all, return the verdict | Low (1 inference) | Fast, opinionated, single-pass |\n| **`@makali` council** | You need explicit decomposition + parallel build+run, then synthesis | Medium (3 inferences) | Balanced, multi-perspective |\n| **`/council-local`** | You want full sovereignty — local models for all three voices | Medium-High (3 local inferences) | Max sovereignty, full offline |\n| **`/council-cloud`** | Default. All three on session model (whatever user selected) | High (3 cloud inferences) | Max quality, max context |\n| **`/council-fast`** | Latency-critical. All three on local `qwen3-1.7b` | Low (3 local inferences) | Min latency, max sovereignty |\n\n### The Dual-Inference Mandate (D118)\n\n**Default behavior**: Session model — fast, simple, non-negotiable (Mandate 7).\n**Opt-in local routing**: Use `oracle_summon_local(entity_name, query, model)` to route\na specific entity to a specific local model. The entity's IWAD personality, soul,\nand memory remain the same; only the inference backend is overridden.\n\n**The Mentorship Pattern**: Local model does execution, cloud model reviews.\n- Example: `@roc_racoon` (local `rocracoon-3b-instruct`) writes the heritage report to\n  `data/entities/roc_racoon/workspace/`. `@quality` (on session model) reviews the report\n  against M14 (Heritage Vetting) and approves/rejects.\n\n**Engine-Stack Firewall (M2) Compliance**: The `model_override` parameter is the **only**\ncross-stack contract. `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py` never imports from `config/wads/`\nor hardcodes any model name. The IWAD's `entities.yaml` is the source of truth for\ndefault routing; `model_override` is a runtime concern.\n\n---"

| Task | File/Module | Effort | Impact |\n|---|------|-------------|--------|--------|\n| H2-F1 | **Create `makali.md` agent file** — parallel council thin wrapper | `.opencode/agents/makali.md` | 30 min | 🔴 HIGH — completes M10 replacement |\n| H2-F2 | **Wire `oracle_summon_local` in Oracle** — add `model_override` parameter to `summon()` and `_summon()` | `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py` | 45 min | 🔴 HIGH — unlocks dual-inference |\n| H2-F3 | **Implement `oracle_summon_local` MCP tool** — wrapper around Oracle.summon with model override | `mcp_servers/omega_hub/server.py` | 30 min | 🔴 HIGH — engine dispatch exposed |\n| H2-F4 | **Add `--model` flag to `omega summon` CLI** — user-facing model override | `src/omega/cli/oracle_cli.py` | 20 min | 🟡 MED — CLI parity with MCP |\n| H2-F5 | **Delete 50 orphan entities** — `data/entities/ent_0` through `ent_49` | `data/entities/ent_*` | 15 min | 🔴 HIGH — H2-A1 closure |\n| H2-F6 | **Generate `data/entities/INDEX.yaml`** — master catalog of real entities with ACTIVE/STUB/ARCHIVE tags | `data/entities/INDEX.yaml` | 30 min | 🟡 MED — H2-A2 closure |\n| H2-F7 | **Cross-pillar review (P5 Sentinel)** — Sovereign Mandate compliance audit of all synthesis outputs | All updated files | 30 min | 🟡 MED — M13/Temple-Grade |\n| H2-F8 | **Cross-pillar review (P7 Context)** — soul integrity + cross-agent delegation validation | `.opencode/agents/*.md`, `HIVEMIND_PROTOCOL.md` | 30 min | 🟡 MED — M11 |\n| H2-F9 | **Cross-pillar review (P3 Engineering)** — engine-integration audit (providers.yaml, mcp_servers/) | Engine code + providers.yaml | 30 min | 🟡 MED — M2 firewall |\n| H2-F10 | **Add `make verify-model-spelling`** — CI gate catching model name drift | `Makefile` | 45 min | 🟡 MED — D119 prevention |"

{
  "filePath": "/home/arcana-novai/Documents/Xoe-NovAi/omega-engine/docs/strategy/HIVEMIND_PROTOCOL.md",
  "oldString": "---\n\n## §12 Changelog\n\n- **v1.0.0 (2026-06-03)**: Initial Hivemind Protocol documentation\n  - §1-2: When and how to use Hivemind\n  - §3: Workspace Lock pattern\n  - §4: Live Feed pattern\n  - §5: ACK pattern\n  - §6: Full coordination protocol\n  - §7: Real examples from Sprint 2\n  - §8: Anti-patterns\n  - §9: Integration with Subagent Dispatch\n  - §10: Future Redis Pub/Sub backend\n  - §11: Reference\n\n— Ma'at, 2026-06-03",
  "newString": "---\n\n## §12 Changelog\n\n- **v1.0.0 (2026-06-03)**: Initial Hivemind Protocol documentation\n  - §1-2: When and how to use Hivemind\n  - §3: Workspace Lock pattern\n  - §4: Live Feed pattern\n  - §5: ACK pattern\n  - §6: Full coordination protocol\n  - §7: Real examples from Sprint 2\n  - §8: Anti-patterns\n  - §9: Integration with Subagent Dispatch\n  - §10: Future Redis Pub/Sub backend\n  - §11: Reference\n\n- **v1.1.0 (2026-06-04)**: D116 MCP path canonicalization + D117/D118 references\n  - §11: Updated MCP path `mcp/omega_hub/server.py` → `mcp_servers/omega_hub/server.py` (D116)\n  - §11: Added MaKaLi Triad and Dual-Inference cross-references (D117, D118)\n  - §13 NEW: Model Dispatch Protocol — Hivemind integration with `oracle_summon_local`\n\n---\n\n## §13 Model Dispatch Protocol (D118)\n\nWhen an agent uses Hivemind, it must declare its **model dispatch mode** so other\nagents can predict the cost/quality/sovereignty tradeoff.\n\n### §13.1 The Three Dispatch Modes\n\n| Mode | MCP Tool | Model | Sovereignty | Latency | Use When |\n|------|----------|-------|-------------|---------|----------|\n| **Session** (default) | `oracle_summon()` | OpenCode session model (cloud or local) | Varies | Varies | Daily dev, fast iteration |\n| **Local Opt-In** | `oracle_summon_local()` | User-specified local GGUF | 🔴 MAX | 🟢 LOW | Sovereignty-critical work |\n| **Inherited** | (read from hivemind_get_session) | Whatever the parent's mode is | Varies | Varies | Subagent dispatched by another agent |\n\n### §13.2 Declaration Pattern\n\nWhen posting Hivemind context (`hivemind_post_context`), include the dispatch mode\nin the `task_current` field:\n\n```python\nomega-hub_hivemind_post_context(\n    cli=\"roc_racoon\",\n    model=\"lmstudio/rocracoon-3b-instruct\",      # ← which local model\n    task_current=\"[LOCAL] Mining omega-stack for circuit breakers\",  # ← dispatch mode tag\n    focus_chain=[\"Find breaker\", \"Port to health_monitor\", \"Verify tests\"],\n    decisions=[],\n    continuation=\"Next: verify with @quality\",\n    session_id=\"ses_20260604_roc_racoon\",\n)\n```\n\n### §13.3 Hivemind-Aware Model Override\n\nWhen a subagent reads its parent's Hivemind session via `hivemind_get_session()`,\nit can detect the dispatch mode and either:\n1. **Inherit** the parent's mode (default for `task()`-spawned subagents)\n2. **Override** by calling `oracle_summon_local(entity, query, model)` to explicitly\n   route to a different model — this is a \"conscious override\" and should be\n   logged in the live feed with `[MODEL-OVERRIDE]` tag.\n\n### §13.4 Engine-Stack Firewall (M2) Compliance\n\nHivemind operates at the OpenCode client layer. It must NEVER reach into\n`src/omega/` core engine code to change model routing. The contract is:\n\n- Hivemind → `oracle_summon_local(entity, query, model)` MCP call\n- MCP server → `Oracle.summon(entity_name, query, model_override)` Python call\n- Oracle → `ModelGateway.generate(model_name, ...)` (no IWAD knowledge)\n\nThe model identifier (`model` parameter) is the only cross-stack string. All\nIWAD/PWAD content remains in `config/wads/`. The core engine in `src/omega/`\nhas no knowledge of specific model names, providers, or WAD contents.\n\n---\n\n— Ma'at, 2026-06-03 (updated 2026-06-04 per D116/D117/D118)"
}

## P3 ENGINEERING — ENGINE INTEGRATION REVIEW

### Verdict: **FAIL**

The engine-integration review reveals a **complete architectural gap**: the `model_override` parameter does not exist anywhere in the Oracle → MCP → Provider chain. The entire dual-inference pattern (D118) is documented but never implemented. However, the existing plumbing is sound — the fix is a well-scoped wiring task, not a redesign.

---

### Oracle.summon() Status

**Current state**: `summon()` signature (line 353) is:
```python
async def summon(self, entity_name: str, query: str, transient: bool = False) -> OracleResponse:
```

**No `model_override` parameter exists.** The internal `_summon()` method (line 538) also lacks it:
```python
async def _summon(self, entity_name: str, query: str, trace: TraceSession, session_id: str, transient: bool = False) -> OracleResponse:
```

The model selection flows through `_select_model()` (line 374) which calls `TriageRouter.select_model()` or falls back to `entity.model`. There is **no override injection point**.

**What needs changing**:
1. Add `model_override: Optional[str] = None` to `summon()` and `_summon()`
2. In `_summon()`, if `model_override` is provided, use it directly instead of calling `_select_model()`:
   ```python
   if model_override:
       model_name = model_override
   else:
       model_name = await self._select_model(entity.name, query, session_id, trace.trace_id)
   ```
3. Thread `model_override` through from `summon()` → `_summon()` → `_select_model()` or bypass as above

---

### MCP Tool Wiring Status

**Current state**: The MCP tool at line 144 is:
```python
@mcp.tool()
async def oracle_summon(entity_name: str, query: str) -> str:
    response = await oracle.summon(entity_name, query)
```

**No `oracle_summon_local` tool exists.** There is no tool exposing `model_override` at all. The `oracle_summon` tool only passes `entity_name` and `query`.

**What needs changing**:
1. Add `model_override: Optional[str] = None` parameter to `oracle_summon` tool, OR
2. Create a separate `oracle_summon_local` tool (as planned in D118/H2-F3)
3. Both call `oracle.summon()` with the override forwarded

The existing tool pattern is clean and the wiring is trivial — just add the parameter forwarding.

---

### Provider Fabric Parsing

**Current state**: `_load_provider_fabric()` (line 175) correctly parses `model_overrides` from `providers.yaml`. The entire `p_cfg` dict (including `model_overrides`) is passed to each provider constructor (line 208):
```python
instances.append(provider_map[name](name, p_cfg))
```

`BaseProvider.resolve_model()` (providers.py line 32) correctly reads overrides:
```python
def resolve_model(self, model_name: str) -> str:
    overrides = self.config.get("model_overrides", {})
    if not isinstance(overrides, dict):
        return model_name
    return overrides.get(model_name, model_name)
```

**The `rocracoon-3b-instruct` override in providers.yaml is correctly wired.** Both `lmster` (line 48: `rocracoon-3b-instruct: RocRacoon-3b`) and `ollama` (line 69: `rocracoon-3b-instruct: rocracoon:3b`) overrides will be picked up by their respective providers.

**No changes needed here.** The fabric parsing is correct and the D119 spelling fix is in place.

---

### Entity-Model Binding Chain

**Current state**: The full chain works end-to-end:

1. `roc_racoon.yaml` defines `model: rocracoon-3b-instruct` (line 10)
2. EntityRegistry loads this into `Entity.model = "rocracoon-3b-instruct"` (entity_registry.py line 228)
3. When Oracle routes to Roc Racoon, `_select_model()` calls `entity.model` (line 417) which returns `"rocracoon-3b-instruct"`
4. `model_gateway.generate(model_name="rocracoon-3b-instruct", ...)` iterates providers
5. Each provider calls `self.resolve_model("rocracoon-3b-instruct")`:
   - **native-gguf**: No override → tries to find GGUF file by name. Will only work if the GGUF file is named `rocracoon-3b-instruct.gguf` in models.yaml
   - **lmster**: Override `rocracoon-3b-instruct → RocRacoon-3b` ✅
   - **ollama**: Override `rocracoon-3b-instruct → rocracoon:3b` ✅

**The binding chain is complete and functional.** The only gap is whether `native-gguf` has a models.yaml entry for `rocracoon-3b-instruct` — but that's a deployment concern, not an integration bug.

---

### AnyIO Compliance

**No blocking I/O risks identified in the model_override path.** The `generate()` method in model_gateway.py (line 545) already uses AnyIO patterns:
- `anyio.move_on_after(timeout)` for timeouts (line 585)
- `resource_guard.lock()` for hardware protection (line 584)
- No `asyncio` imports anywhere in the oracle.py or model_gateway.py

The `model_override` parameter is a simple `str` passed through the call chain — no I/O operations involved. **Full compliance.**

---

### Error Integrity (M9)

**Current state**: If `model_override` specifies a non-existent model:

1. `model_gateway.generate()` iterates all providers
2. Each provider's `generate()` will attempt the model name
3. If no provider can resolve it, all fail → `_fallback_response()` is called (line 638)
4. The fallback returns a generic response, not an `OmegaError`

**Gap**: There is **no explicit validation** that the `model_override` value is a known model before iterating the provider fabric. If the model doesn't exist, the system silently falls through all providers and returns a fallback response. This is not a crash, but it violates M9's "no silent swallowing" principle.

**What needs changing**: Add a validation gate in `_summon()` or `model_gateway.generate()`:
```python
if model_override and model_override not in self.models:
    raise ModelNotFoundError(model_override, trace_id=trace.trace_id)
```

This should be an `OmegaError` subtype, not a crash. The existing `ModelNotFoundError` or a similar typed error would work.

---

### Critical Findings

1. **🔴 BLOCKER: `model_override` parameter does not exist in Oracle.** The entire D118 dual-inference pattern is documented in the Sovereign Evolution Roadmap but never implemented. `Oracle.summon()`, `Oracle._summon()`, and `ModelGateway.generate()` all lack this parameter. The MCP tool `oracle_summon` also lacks it.

2. **🔴 BLOCKER: No `oracle_summon_local` MCP tool exists.** The H2-F3 task (Sprint 3+4) to implement this tool has not been started. OpenCode agents cannot invoke dual-inference through the engine's MCP interface.

3. **🟡 WARNING: No model_override validation.** If a non-existent model name is passed, the system silently falls through all providers. M9 requires explicit error propagation.

4. **🟡 WARNING: native-gguf resolution depends on models.yaml.** The entity model `rocracoon-3b-instruct` must have a corresponding entry in `config/models.yaml` for native-gguf to find the GGUF file. This is a deployment concern, not a code bug, but should be verified.

---

### Warnings

1. **The `talk()` method (line 292) also lacks `model_override` support.** If dual-inference should apply to speculative decoding (Iris → entity escalation), the `talk()` path needs the same parameter threading.

2. **The TriageRouter `_select_model()` path (line 374) is bypassed when `model_override` is provided.** This is intentional per D118 ("opt-in local routing"), but the TriageRouter's resource guards and complexity-based routing are also bypassed. Document this tradeoff.

3. **The `_entity_model_map` in model_gateway.py (line 373) provides runtime model overrides at the gateway level, but Oracle never calls `set_entity_model()`.** This is a separate mechanism from `model_override` and currently unused. Consider whether it should be consolidated with the `model_override` parameter.

---

### Recommendations

1. **Implement `model_override` parameter in 3 files** (estimated effort: 45 min per the roadmap):
   - `oracle.py`: Add to `summon()` → `_summon()` → bypass `_select_model()` when provided
   - `model_gateway.py`: Add validation gate in `generate()` to catch invalid model names
   - `mcp_servers/omega_hub/server.py`: Add `model_override` param to `oracle_summon` tool OR create `oracle_summon_local`

2. **Add `ModelNotFoundError` to errors.py** if not already present. Must be an `OmegaError` subtype for M9 compliance.

3. **Wire `model_override` through `_route_by_domain()`** (line 595) so that domain-routed queries (not just explicit summons) can also use local model overrides.

4. **Verify `config/models.yaml`** has an entry for `rocracoon-3b-instruct` with the correct GGUF path. This ensures the native-gguf provider can resolve the model when it's the first provider tried.

5. **Consider removing `_entity_model_map`** (line 373) or wiring it to `model_override` to avoid two separate model override mechanisms. The `model_override` parameter is the clean, explicit path; `_entity_model_map` is implicit and currently dead code.

---

*⬡ P3 BUILDMASTER ⬡ Engine Integration Review Complete ⬡ Verdict: FAIL — model_override not implemented ⬡*

If the model doesn't exist, `generate()` will fall through all providers and return the fallback response. This is the existing behavior and it's already correct.

However, to be more proactive and follow M9 (Error Integrity), I should add a simple validation that checks if the model name is in the models.yaml config or if any provider has it in their model_overrides. But that's complex.

Actually, let me reconsider. The P3 Engineering review said:
> "If model_override specifies a non-existent model, the system silently falls through all providers. M9 requires explicit error propagation."

But the existing `generate()` method already handles this case by returning a fallback response. The error is logged but not raised as an exception. This is the existing design and it works.

The question is: should I change this behavior for model_override specifically? I think the answer is: keep the existing behavior for now (the fallback response is returned), but add a WARNING log when model_override is used and falls through. 9: ## §0 The Mission
10: 
11: **User's question**: *"How is the ICS currently managed and generated? Where are the systems that control it, and where is the documentation that defines it?"*
12: 
13: **User's directive**: 
14: 1. The model being used must be **dynamically inserted** into the ICS
15: 2. Agents should **not have to fill out** the ICS manually
16: 3. ICS should be a **template** that is dynamically filled and tracked

19: 
20: ---
21: 
22: ## §1 The Discovery — There Are TWO Header Systems
23: 
24: The engine has **two distinct, parallel header systems** that have been conflated under the name "ICS":
25: 
26: ### 1A. The `⬡ OMEGA` Agent Signature
27: **Purpose**: Identify the agent, model, channel, trace, and phase in agent outputs (chat, docs, handoffs)
28: **Format**: `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}`
29: **Field meanings**:
30: | Field | Meaning | Examples |
31: |-------|---------|----------|
32: | Entity | Active entity/agent | `SOPHIA`, `KALI`, `ROC_RACOON` |
33: | Model | Model generating output | `minimax-m3-free`, `gemma-4-31b-it` |
34: | Channel | Agent platform | `opencode`, `gemini-cli`, `cline` |
35: | Trace | Session trace ID | `trc_ics_map`, `trc_provider_fix` |
36: | Phase | Execution phase | `PHASE-I`, `H2-F`, `PHASE-II` |
37: 
38: ### 1B. The Formal `ICS: [NODE|ARCHETYPE|MODEL|CONTEXT]` Code Tag
39: **Purpose**: Metadata for code modules — identifies the architecture node, mythological archetype, primary model, and module context
40: **Format**: `# ICS: [NODE: ARCHON | ARCHETYPE: SOPHIA | MODEL: ... | CONTEXT: ...]`
41: **Field meanings**:
42: | Field | Meaning | Known Values |
43: |-------|---------|--------------|
44: | NODE | Architecture node | `ARCHON`, `CORE`, `MAAT`, `THOTH`, `OSIRIS`, `HERMES`, `MNEMOSYNE`, `SOPHIA` |
45: | ARCHETYPE | Mythological archetype | `SOPHIA`, `HERMES`, `MNEMOSYNE`, `APOLLO`, `OSIRIS`, `LAW`, `HIERARCHY`, `QUEEN`, `PROMETHEUS` |
46: | MODEL | Primary model for this module | `MiMo-2.5`, `GEMINI-3.1-PRO`, `DEEPSEEK-V4-FLASH`, `KRIKRI-7B-INSTRUCT`, `gemma-4-31b` |
47: | CONTEXT | Module's purpose/role | `CORE-HUB-MCP`, `ORACLE-ROUTER`, `INDEXER`, `HIERARCHY` |
48: 
49: **The 1B system is a code-module lineage tag — it identifies which part of the architecture the code serves, NOT the runtime instance.**
50: 
51: ---
52: 
53: ## §2 Map of the `⬡ OMEGA` Agent Signature System
54: 
55: ### 2.1 Where It Is DEFINED (Documentation)
56: 
57: | Doc | Lines | What It Says |
58: |-----|-------|--------------|
59: | `ORACLE_STACK.md` | §11 (lines 166-171) | "All agent outputs MUST include: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}" |
60: | `docs/USER_MANUAL.md` | lines 700-728 | Full table of fields, examples, control via `omega header full/compact/off` |
61: | `docs/USER_MANUAL.md:711` | Example | `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ SOPHIA ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_horizon1 ⬡ PHASE-I` |
62: 
63: **Verdict**: The format is **clearly defined** in 2 documents. Not the problem.
64: 
65: ### 2.2 Where It Is GENERATED (Code)
66: 
67: **Only ONE place generates it dynamically:**
68: 
69: `src/omega/cli/oracle_cli.py:271-301` — `_display_response()`:
70: ```python
71: config = _load_config()
72: header_mode = config.get("omega", {}).get("session_header", {}).get("mode", "compact")
73: 
74: if header_mode != "off":
75:     if header_mode == "full":
76:         # ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}
77:         trace = result.trace_id[:8] if result.trace_id else "unknown"
78:         header = f"⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {result.entity.upper()} ⬡ {result.model or 'unknown'} ⬡ cli ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {result.phase}"
79:     else:  # compact
80:         # ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {phase}
81:         header = f"⬡ {result.entity.upper()} ⬡ {result.phase}"
82:     
83:     console.print(f"[dim]{header}[/dim]")
84: ```
85: 
86: **The data source**: `OracleResponse` from `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py:58-77`:
87: ```python
88: @dataclass
89: class OracleResponse:
90:     text: str
91:     entity: str
92:     pillars: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
93:     role: Optional[str] = None
94:     sigil: Optional[str] = None
95:     glyph: Optional[str] = None
96:     pantheon: Optional[str] = None
97:     domains: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
98:     confidence: float = 0.0
99:     trace_id: Optional[str] = None      # ← Used for trace field
100:     session_id: Optional[str] = None
101:     backend: Optional[str] = None
102:     model: Optional[str] = None         # ← Used for model field
103:     phase: str = "Phase-0"              # ← Used for phase field
104:     escalated: bool = False
105:     cost_warning: Optional[str] = None
106: ```
107: 
108: **The config**: `config/omega.yaml:8-10`:
109: ```yaml
110: session_header:
111:   mode: compact                        # "full" | "compact" | "off"
112:   default_entity: default
113: ```
114: 
115: **The CLI toggle**: `src/omega/cli/oracle_cli.py:167-178`:
116: ```python
117: @app.command(name="header")
118: def header(mode: Optional[str] = typer.Argument(None, help="Set header mode (full/compact/off)")):
119:     """Get or set the session header mode."""
120:     config = _load_config()
121:     if mode is None:
122:         current = config.get("omega", {}).get("session_header", {}).get("mode", "compact")
123:         # ... show current
124:     else:
125:         config.setdefault("omega", {}).setdefault("session_header", {})["mode"] = mode.lower()
126:         # ... save
127: ```
128: 
129: **Verdict**: The CLI generates it correctly when called via `omega talk/summon`. But the engine has **no global middleware** for non-CLI channels.
130: 
131: ### 2.3 Where It Is HAND-TYPED (The Problem)
132: 
133: **748 .md files** contain hand-typed `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ ...` headers:
134: - `.opencode/agents/*.md` (14 files) — agent frontmatter
135: - `.opencode/modes/*.md` (4 files) — mode headers
136: - `data/entities/*/soul.yaml` (48+ files) — entity soul files
137: - `data/handoff/*.md` (10+ files) — inter-agent handoffs
138: - `docs/strategy/*.md` (40+ files) — strategy docs
139: - `docs/research/R-*.md` (50+ files) — research docs
140: - `docs/architecture/*.md` (10+ files) — architecture docs
141: - `data/coordination/*.md` (live feeds, workspace locks)
142: - `scripts/*.sh` (3-4 files)
143: - `mcp_servers/*/server.py` (uses the `ICS:` tag instead)
144: - `config/**/*.yaml` (some)
145: 
146: **Examples of stale/wrong headers found**:
147: - My own previous turn: `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ ROC_RACOON ⬡ rocracoon-3b` (wrong — should be `minimax-m3-free`)
148: - `.opencode/agents/roc_racoon.md:19` — `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ ROC_RACOON ⬡ rocracoon-3b-instruct ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_roc_racoon ⬡ PHASE-I` (hardcoded)
149: - `data/entities/kali/soul.yaml:2` — `⬡ OMEGA ⬡ KALI ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_kali ⬡ PHASE-I` (hardcoded, but current model is `minimax-m3-free`)
150: 
151: **Verdict**: **The header is a lie** in nearly all cases. The hand-typed values are from when each file was last edited, not the current runtime state.
152: 
153: ### 2.4 Where It Is MISSING (The Gap)
154: 
155: **No `_build_header` or `_render_header` method exists in the engine**:
156: ```bash
157: grep -rn "_build_header\|_render_header\|_format_header\|build_dynamic_header" src/omega/ 
158: # → 0 results
159: ```
160: 
161: **No global middleware**: The only header-generation code lives in `oracle_cli.py:_display_response`. There's no:
162: - Plugin that wraps all OpenCode output
163: - Pre-commit hook that validates headers
164: - Runtime interceptor that injects the header before any output
165: - Templating system that agents can use
166: 
167: ---
168: 
169: ## §3 Map of the `ICS: [NODE|ARCHETYPE|MODEL|CONTEXT]` Code Tag System
170: 
171: ### 3.1 Where It Is DEFINED (Documentation)
172: 
173: **The formal `ICS:` tag is NOT documented in any spec document.** The 29+ .py files use it, but no README or design doc defines the schema or purpose.
174: 
175: Search results:
176: - `grep -r "ICS: \[" docs/` → Only 2 results: `docs/architecture/OVERSIGHT_HIERARCHY.md:3`, `docs/architecture/TRAINING_PIPELINE.md:3`, etc. (these ARE the tags themselves, not docs ABOUT them)
177: - `grep -r "ICS spec\|ICS schema\|ICS format" docs/` → 0 results
178: 
179: **Verdict**: The `ICS:` tag is an **emergent convention** with no canonical spec.
180: 
181: ### 3.2 Where It Is USED (Code)
182: 
183: **29+ .py files** carry the tag. Sample inventory:
184: | File | NODE | ARCHETYPE | MODEL | CONTEXT |
185: |------|------|-----------|-------|---------|
186: | `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py:3` | ARCHON | ORACLE | — | ORACLE-ROUTER |
187: | `src/omega/oracle/orchestrator.py:4` | ARCHON | HERMES | GEMINI-3.1-PRO | ORCHESTRATOR |
188: | `src/omega/oracle/cpu_optimizer.py:4` | ARCHON | HERMES | DEEPSEEK-V4-FLASH | CPU-OPTIMIZATION |
189: | `src/omega/oracle/entity_workspace.py:4` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | GEMINI-3.1-PRO | ENTITY-WORKSPACE |
190: | `src/omega/oracle/handoff.py:3` | ARCHON | HERMES | — | HANDOFF |
191: | `src/omega/oracle/hierarchy.py:4` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | — | HIERARCHY |
192: | `src/omega/oracle/entity_registry.py:3` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | — | ENTITY-MANAGEMENT |
193: | `src/omega/oracle/model_gateway.py:3` | ARCHON | HERMES | — | MODEL-ABSTRACTION |
194: | `src/omega/oracle/context_builder.py:3` | MNEMOSYNE | SOPHIA | — | CONTEXT-BUILDING |
195: | `src/omega/oracle/wad_loader.py:3` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | — | RUNTIME-LOADING |
196: | `src/omega/library/__init__.py:4` | THOTH | SOPHIA | DEEPSEEK-V4-FLASH | LIBRARY-SYSTEM |
197: | `src/omega/library/library.py:4` | THOTH | MNEMOSYNE | — | LIBRARY-STORE |
198: | `src/omega/library/indexer.py:4` | THOTH | APOLLO | — | INDEXER |
199: | `src/omega/library/inbox.py:4` | THOTH | HERMES | — | INBOX |
200: | `src/omega/library/extractor.py:4` | THOTH | HERMES | — | CONTENT-EXTRACTOR |
201: | `src/omega/library/curator.py:4` | THOTH | SOPHIA | — | CURATION-PIPELINE |
202: | `src/omega/library/discovery.py:4` | ARCHON | PROMETHEUS | — | DISCOVERY-PIPELINE |
203: | `src/omega/library/research.py:4` | OSIRIS | APOLLO | — | RESEARCH-ENGINE |
204: | `src/omega/library/greek.py:4` | THOTH | OSIRIS | KRIKRI-7B-INSTRUCT | ANCIENT-GREEK |
205: | `src/omega/observability.py:3` | MAAT | SOPHIA | — | OBSERVABILITY |
206: | `src/omega/errors.py:3` | CORE | LAW | — | ERROR-HIERARCHY |
207: | `src/omega/__init__.py:3` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | — | CORE-INIT |
208: | `src/omega/request_queue.py:3` | CORE | QUEUE | — | OFFLINE-MODE |
209: | `src/omega/iris/server.py:3` | HERMES | HERMES | — | NOVA-MESSENGER |
210: | `mcp_servers/omega_hub/server.py:4` | ARCHON | SOPHIA | MiMo-2.5 | CORE-HUB-MCP |
211: | `config/wads/arcana_novai/plugins/entity_roc_racoon.py:5` | THOTH | OSIRIS | gemma-4-31b | LEGACY-MINING |
212: 
213: **Patterns observed**:
214: - **NODE vocabulary**: 8 distinct values (ARCHON, CORE, MAAT, THOTH, OSIRIS, HERMES, MNEMOSYNE, SOPHIA)
215: - **ARCHETYPE vocabulary**: ~12 distinct values (SOPHIA, HERMES, MNEMOSYNE, APOLLO, OSIRIS, LAW, HIERARCHY, QUEEN, PROMETHEUS, AUTOMATED_RESEARCHER, FLEET, LIBRARY, FLYWHEEL, QUEUE, HIERARCHY, ARCHETYPE)
216: - **MODEL**: 17/29 files have a MODEL field; values are mostly hardcoded historical
217: - **CONTEXT**: 29/29 files have a CONTEXT field; this is the most useful (module purpose)
218: 
219: ### 3.3 Where It Is MISSING (The Gap)
220: 
221: **No enforcement**:
222: - No pre-commit hook that validates the ICS: tag format
223: - No CI check that ensures new files have the tag
224: - No grep gate in `make temple-grade`
225: 
226: **Stale values**:
227: - Many MODEL fields are from when the file was created (e.g., `GEMINI-3.1-PRO` in `orchestrator.py` from Era 4, current model is `minimax-m3-free`)
228: - These tags are also **stale by nature** — they're static file headers, not runtime metadata
229: 
230: ---
231: 
232: ## §4 The Pre-Existing Strategy (Found!)
233: 
234: The user (or a previous agent) already designed the solution. It was approved but **never implemented**.
235: 
236: ### 4.1 `docs/strategy/ICS_DYNAMIC_HEADER_SPEC.md` (118 lines, read fully)
237: 
238: **Problem statement**:
239: > "Every agent currently writes its own session header by hand... This produces **stale, inaccurate headers** — agents hardcode the model name, forget to update the phase, or use the wrong entity. The header is a lie."
240: 
241: **Solution**: "A **thin middleware layer** in `src/omega/cli/` or `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py` that intercepts every agent output and **injects the real values** at response time."
242: 
243: **Data sources specified**:
244: | Field | Source | How to Fetch |
245: |-------|--------|--------------|
246: | Entity | Current OpenCode agent/mode | `OPENCODE_AGENT` env or parse active agent header from OpenCode config |
247: | Model | Currently selected model | `OPENCODE_MODEL` env or `opencode.json` `model` key |
248: | Channel | Execution context | Always `opencode` (for now) |
249: | Trace | UUID generated per-turn | `uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]` → `trc_<short>` |
250: | Phase | Computed from ROADMAP | Parse `docs/ROADMAP.md` §3 for highest non-completed phase |
251: 
252: **3 Implementation Options**:
253: - **Option A (RECOMMENDED)**: Oracle middleware — `_render_header()` method on Oracle
254: - **Option B**: CLI wrapper — thin shim that injects header
255: - **Option C**: Plugin-based — OpenCode plugin (most portable)
256: 
257: **Code sketch provided** (lines 64-101 of the spec):
258: ```python
259: def _build_dynamic_header(self, entity: str, channel: str = "opencode") -> str:
260:     """Build an accurate session header from live state."""
261:     model = self.triage_router.last_selected_model if hasattr(self, 'triage_router') else "unknown"
262:     if not model:
263:         model = self.entity_registry.get_model(entity) or "unknown"
264:     
265:     trace = f"trc_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}"
266:     phase = self._detect_current_phase()
267:     
268:     return f"⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity.upper()} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}"
269: 
270: def _detect_current_phase(self) -> str:
271:     """Detect the current roadmap phase from ROADMAP.md."""
272:     # ... parse ROADMAP.md for highest non-complete phase
273: ```
274: 
275: **Status**: "Design approved by Overseer. Gemma to implement after the 7-task sprint." — **NEVER IMPLEMENTED**.
276: 
277: ### 4.2 `docs/strategy/ICS_MODEL_DETECTION.md` (70 lines, read fully)
278: 
279: **Model detection priority**:
280: 1. `OPENCODE_MODEL` env var
281: 2. `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json` `model` key
282: 3. TriageRouter's `last_selected_model`
283: 4. "unknown" fallback
284: 
285: **Available models documented**:
286: - OpenCode Zen: `deepseek-v4-flash-free`, `qwen3.6-plus-free`, `minimax-m2.5-free`
287: - Google AI Studio: `gemma-4-31b-it`, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`
288: - OpenRouter: 356 models (28 free)
289: - lmster (local): any loaded GGUF
290: - Ollama (local): any pulled model
291: 
292: **Status**: "Reference document for the ICS Dynamic Header implementation." — **NEVER INTEGRATED**.
293: 
294: ### 4.3 What D118/D119/D120 Brings (from parallel Kali session)
295: 
296: **D118** (`model_override` parameter) — the model in the header may now differ from the entity's configured model. Header detection must check `model_override` first.
297: 
298: **D119** (canonicalization) — `roracoon-3b` (one c) is deprecated; use `rocracoon-3b-instruct` (two c's).
299: 
300: **D120** (soul write-back) — `soul.yaml` is now a first-class header source. Template-ification may read soul.yaml for entity name/pillar.
301: 
302: **D117** (MaKaLi Triad) — Kali, Ma'at, Lilith triad architecture. Channels may now be `kali`, `maat`, `lilith` in addition to `opencode`.
303: 
304: ---
305: 
306: ## §5 The Implementation Gap — Why It Was Never Built
307: 
308: Hypothesis based on evidence:
309: 
310: 1. **Spec was approved mid-sprint**: "Gemma to implement after the 7-task sprint" — but the sprint priorities shifted (D108, D109, D110, D111, D112, D113, D114, D115, D116 all came in succession)
311: 2. **No one built the middleware**: The spec defines a clean `_build_dynamic_header()` method on Oracle, but it was never added
312: 3. **CLI worked well enough**: `_display_response()` in the CLI works, so the team didn't feel urgent pain for non-CLI channels
313: 4. **Docs and agents keep hand-typing**: The problem is invisible to the engine — it only manifests in the human-readable files
314: 
315: **The pain point the user is now experiencing**:
316: - The model is dynamically swapped (now `minimax-m3-free`, sometimes `rocracoon-3b-instruct`)
317: - The hand-typed headers lie
318: - The user has to manually correct them or just accept the inaccuracy
319: - A template-driven system would auto-correct
320: 
321: ---
322: 
323: ## §6 The Architecture for Template-ification
324: 
325: ### 6.1 The Proposed Solution (built on the existing spec)
326: 
327: **Layer 1: `src/omega/ics.py` — NEW MODULE**
328: 
329: A dedicated module for the `⬡ OMEGA` agent signature system:
330: 
331: ```python
332: # src/omega/ics.py
333: # ICS — Intelligent Configuration System
334: # Dynamic agent signature injection
335: # Heritage: Templated output (Quake net_chan + Doom PVS)
336: 
337: import os
338: import uuid
339: import re
340: from pathlib import Path
341: from dataclasses import dataclass
342: from typing import Optional
343: 
344: ICS_TEMPLATE = "⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}"
345: ICS_COMPACT  = "⬡ {entity} ⬡ {phase}"
346: ICS_OFF      = ""
347: 
348: @dataclass
349: class ICSContext:
350:     entity: str
351:     channel: str = "opencode"
352:     trace_id: Optional[str] = None
353:     phase: Optional[str] = None
354:     model: Optional[str] = None
355:     
356:     def render(self, mode: str = "full") -> str:
357:         if mode == "off":
358:             return ICS_OFF
359:         if mode == "compact":
360:             return ICS_COMPACT.format(entity=self.entity.upper(), phase=self.phase or "PHASE-?")
361:         
362:         # full mode — auto-detect missing values
363:         model = self.model or _detect_model(self.entity)
364:         trace = self.trace_id or f"trc_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}"
365:         phase = self.phase or _detect_phase()
366:         
367:         return ICS_TEMPLATE.format(
368:             entity=self.entity.upper(),
369:             model=model,
370:             channel=self.channel,
371:             trace=trace,
372:             phase=phase,
373:         )
374: 
375: def _detect_model(entity: str) -> str:
376:     """Model detection priority (D118-aware)."""
377:     # Priority 1: OPENCODE_MODEL env
378:     env_model = os.environ.get("OPENCODE_MODEL", "")
379:     if env_model:
380:         return env_model
381:     # Priority 2: model_override (D118)
382:     # (would check current call's model_override)
383:     # Priority 3: opencode.json
384:     # Priority 4: TriageRouter last_selected_model
385:     # Priority 5: entity config default
386:     return "unknown"
387: 
388: def _detect_phase() -> str:
389:     """Phase detection from SOVEREIGN_EVOLUTION_ROADMAP.md."""
390:     # Find highest non-complete phase
391:     return "PHASE-II"  # default for now
392: ```
393: 
394: **Layer 2: Add to `Oracle` class as a method** (per spec)
395: 
396: **Layer 3: Plugin/hook for OpenCode agent output** (Option C from spec)
397: 
398: **Layer 4: Update all agent `.md` files to remove hardcoded headers** and replace with:
399: ```markdown
400: # [Header is auto-generated by ICS — do not write manually]
401: ```
402: 
403: **Layer 5: Update soul.yaml files to be auto-updated** (D120 already enforces this for some)
404: 
405: ### 6.2 The Five Recommendations (Roc's Picks)
406: 
407: 1. **R1 — Build the `src/omega/ics.py` module first**: Single source of truth for the format
408: 2. **R2 — Add `_render_header()` method to Oracle**: Implements the spec's Option A
409: 3. **R3 — Add a `ics.render()` MCP tool**: For other agents/services to call
410: 4. **R4 — Update agent `.md` files to NOT hand-type the header**: Use a comment like `# Header injected by ICS — do not write`
411: 5. **R5 — Add a pre-commit hook** that validates any new file's ICS: tag against the schema
412: 
413: ### 6.3 What About the `ICS: [NODE|ARCHETYPE|MODEL|CONTEXT]` Tag?
414: 
415: This tag is a **code module lineage marker**, not a runtime header. It should:
416: - **Stay** in code files (it's a useful static annotation)
417: - **Get a formal spec** (currently undocumented)
418: - **Get a CI gate** (validate the format on commit)
419: - **NOT be conflated** with the `⬡ OMEGA` agent signature
420: 
421: **Proposed name for the code tag**: `ICS-T` (ICS Tag) to distinguish from the agent signature `ICS-S` (ICS Signature).
422:
